- **research-runner**: Long-running research passes over many URLs or HN items; writes raw artifacts then hands off to wiki-ingest patterns. ([SkinnnyJay/wiki-llm](https://github.com/SkinnnyJay/wiki-llm/blob/HEAD/agents/research-runner.md))
- **research-runner**: Reports empirical metrics for a baseline or candidate plugin against a fixture, given runtime-injected role-instance variables and verb-tool bindings. Spawned by the research-first build-prd variant in parallel — once per role-instance (baseline, candidate, ...) — with the same identity but different injected context. - **research-scout**: Answers questions about an API, library version, CLI flag, config option, or current best practice by checking the installed environment first and then version-matched official docs — never from training memory alone. Delegate to research-scout before relying on any version-sensitive external fact, or when the user asks "what's the latest / correct way to do X". Requires the specific question and, ideally, the project it applies to. Returns the answer with source, version, and date, labelling anything unverified. Verdicts each finding COPY/LEARN-FROM/AVOID against a verified license. Never writes code or files issues. Not for market/competitor facts (use market-analyst). - **research-scout**: Independently researches a competitive and design landscape — competitor behavior, category conventions, and the opening. Run once during direction before it is committed. - **research-scout**: Research specialist — finds and summarizes proven entity-resolution / record-linkage techniques (papers, Kaggle solutions, libraries), checks model and library licenses (must be MIT/Apache-2.0, ≤8B params for models), and turns findings into ranked, concrete experiment proposals. Never fetches external datasets. ([Aryan-Bhendarkar/Amazon-ML-Challenge-2026](https://github.com/Aryan-Bhendarkar/Amazon-ML-Challenge-2026/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/research-scout.md) · ★1)
- **research-scout**: Use BEFORE brainstorming or planning ONLY when the task sits in the large/risky tier of the kit-workflow sizing rules — unfamiliar libraries, security/auth, performance-critical paths, or novel architecture. NOT for small tasks or bug fixes. Does web research with WebSearch/WebFetch and returns a structured research summary. Does NOT write code, does NOT review code — research only. Works in every profile. ([cholateio/multi-agent-starter](https://github.com/cholateio/multi-agent-starter/blob/HEAD/plugin/agents/research-scout.md) · ★1 · 2 copies)
- **research-scout**: Investigates prior art, existing solutions in the codebase, compound knowledge docs, libraries, and community approaches before building anything new. ([dlabs/claude-marketplace](https://github.com/dlabs/claude-marketplace/blob/HEAD/blueprint-dev/agents/research-scout.md) · ★1)
- **research-scout**: Spawned by /research-analyst to run ONE research angle thoroughly and independently. Blind to other scouts — this is the wisdom-of-the-crowd fan-out. Returns findings, sources, and per-claim confidence so the analyst can aggregate on agreement and investigate divergence. ([ggoosen/Digital-Workforce](https://github.com/ggoosen/Digital-Workforce/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/research-scout.md) · ★1)
- **research-scout**: Read-only scientific-retrieval and credibility-grading agent (DISCOVER stage of the scriptorium lifecycle). Retrieves literature from tiered sources (PubMed, OpenAlex, Crossref, Europe PMC, Semantic Scholar, arXiv/bioRxiv/medRxiv, HuggingFace papers), deduplicates by DOI, resolves legal open-access full text (Unpaywall / OpenAlex), grades each finding on a graduated epistemic scale, optionally compares findings against the user's knowledge base, and returns a findings table + draft note bullets. Examples:\n- &lt;example&gt;\n Context: User needs comprehensive paper search.\n user: "Search Semantic Scholar for recent transformer architecture papers"\n assistant: "I'll use the semantic-scholar-api agent to search for recent transformer papers with detailed metadata."\n &lt;commentary&gt;\n Semantic Scholar search needed, perfect for semantic-scholar-api agent.\n &lt;/commentary&gt;\n&lt;/example&gt; ([liq22/Claude_Code_for_Research_Config](https://github.com/liq22/Claude_Code_for_Research_Config/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/research-semantic-scholar.md) · ★1)
- **research-sensor**: The SENSE organ of the Claudify Analyst engine: pulls the run's shared raw data ONCE and writes a provenance-tagged snapshot every specialist reads, instead of each specialist re-fetching the same pages. The efficiency law: pull once, feed many. It also tags each fetched item with its retrieval pass (which search framing found it), which is what makes the RAS saturation estimate (capture-recapture across independent passes) computable. It fetches and files; it never interprets, scores, or judges. ([gannino/your-assistant](https://github.com/gannino/your-assistant/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/research-sensor.md))
- **research-sidecar**: On-demand research sub-agent for inline topic investigation. Use when a calling agent (typically Rubber Ducky during `/rubber-ducky` or Product Manager during `/create-prd` discovery / critical review) hits a fuzzy claim or open question that needs grounding in current sources without polluting the caller's context. Returns a short decision-grade synthesis (bottom-line + key findings + sources + caveats) plus a structured `NEED_CLARIFICATION` envelope when the question is too underspecified to answer. Designs, writes, tests and maintains the project's code — data pipelines, reference methods and baselines, evaluation and metrics, simulations, benchmark harnesses, plotting and figure tooling, and the tests for all of it — following the language's standards, SOLID principles and reproducibility practice. - **research-solution-architect**: Hard algorithmic and performance-critical design. Formalizes the problem (inputs, outputs, invariants, scale, explicit complexity targets), surveys prior art (papers, known algorithms, data structures, production libraries), adapts an existing solution if one genuinely fits, otherwise designs from first principles with complexity analysis, a correctness argument, edge-case handling, a working prototype, and benchmarks.
